### pair programming

Much research is done about pair programming as an educational tool to improve teaching and learning. It is a very good strategy to implement in both computational thinking and coding. Even in spreadsheets it can work when teaching functions.

Pair programming is when two learners sit in front of the computer a work together to solve a certain problem or complete a certain task. Each one of the learners in the pair has their distinctive task what they have to do during the problem solving process. One learner is called the driver and the other learner is called the navigator.

Pair programming can be compared with rally drivers. The person behind the steering wheel is the driver and the person sitting next to him/her is the navigator. The driver holds the steering wheel (pencil, mouse and/or keyboard) and is in control of the car (project). The navigator has the map (resources, like textbook) in front of him/her to determine the shortest and most logic path to arrive at the end (output). The navigator watches the time to make sure everything happens according to time. Both the driver and navigator must have trust in each other to make sure the get to the end save and sound.

### advantages of pair progamming

Programming errors are limited

Due to the reason that learners evaluate each other the whole time programming errors are limited and better projects are produced.

Study behaviour improves

Learners start enjoying these type of programming and want to learn more about programming. They even start to apply pair work to other subjects.

Self-confidence are improved

Most of the time the program works when coding in pairs and the confidence of both learners improve and they become more certain that they are able to do programming.

Problem solving skills improve

Learners guide each other through the problem solving process and learn from each other different methods to solve a problem.

Before explaining the roles it is important to know that the roles may change during an activity.

The work of the teacher decreases

Learners teach each other to do coding and teacher do not have to do the explanation all by themselves.

### disadvantages of pair programming

There is not a lot of disadvantages with pair programming. The most common one is that roles are not rotated, and sometimes knowledge is not transferred between pairs.

### roles during problem solving

The driver will have the pen in his/her hand where he/she will write down the 4 techniques of the problem solving process while the navigator will instruct the driver what to write down while looking at resources on how to implement the 4 techniques the correct way. The navigator will also evaluate each step in the algorithm to make sure the sequence is correct and that the algorithm will do what it is intended to do.

### roles during coding

The driver will have the keyboard and mouse in front of him/her and will code according to the instructions of the navigator. The navigator will tell the drive which blocks to use and where to find them. The navigator now has the algorithm in hand and make sure the driver codes according to the plan (algorithm). The navigator can still look for better way to complete the project more efficient.

### grouping of pairs

The question is always been asked by the teacher “how will I pair them?” Here is some explanation on how pairing can be done with different type of programmers.

The expert-expert pair

An expert is a person who are very knowledgeable about or skilful in a particular area. This group can be of value when a difficult problem need to be solved. One of the advantages of this type of pairing is that the two experts can motivate each other to become better programmers.

The expert-average pair

The average person is one who is on an ordinary level. The average programmers have the ability to do coding, but not on the same level as the expert. The average programmer can be an expert in making, with the correct guidance. Pairing an average programmer with an expert can assist in this reaching this purpose.

The expert-novice pair

The purpose of this pairing is when the expert must learn the novice to program. Many times when peers teach other they understand something faster. The downfall to this type of pairing is that experts most of the coding do and do not take the opportunity to teach the novice programmer.

The novice-novice pair

This type of pairing can work when a very easy problem needed to be solved. The problem here is that learners may not communicate as often as they have to and they do not always have the ability to see when an error is in the algorithm and/or coding.

### the role as teacher in pair programming

The teacher’s role cannot be over emphasized in pair programming.

1. Convince learners the value of pair programming

Most learners are against group work, especially you clever learner, because the always feel they do all the work and the others do not do anything. Explain to learners that in pair programming it is not the situation and that both learners must take part in the process.

2. Define the roles for each learner

It is very important to define each learner’s role in pair programming. Good clear guidance must be given.

3. Manage pair programming

Decide which learners must pair together.

4. Rotate the roles

Make sure that rotation of roles do take place. Make learners stand up and swap chairs

### TemplatesStorage.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 1 Systems Technology Data/Ch1Files/TemplatesStorage.docx`

Telling Word 2019 where templates are stored

The first time you create a template, Word stores it in this folder:

- C:\Users\Username\Documents\Custom Office Templates

However, in previous versions of Word, templates were stored in one of these folders:

- C:\Users\Owner\Documents\Custom Office Templates

- C:\Users\Username\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\Templates

To keep all your templates in one place, either move them to the C:\Users\Username\Documents\Custom Office Templates folder or tell Word where you prefer to store templates.

Follow these steps to tell Word 2019 where you keep templates on your computer:

1. On the File tab, choose Options.
The Word Options dialog box opens.

2. Go to the Save category.

3. In the Default Personal Templates Location text box, enter the path to the folder where you prefer to store templates.

4. Click OK.

### Activity 2_7.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 2 Keyboarding Data Files/Activity 2_7.docx`

Video provides a powerful way to help you prove your point. When you click Online Video, you can paste in the embed code for the video you want to add. You can also type a keyword to search online for the video that best fits your document.

To make your document look professionally produced, Word provides header, footer, cover page, and text box designs that complement each other. For example, you can add a matching cover page, header, and sidebar. Click Insert and then choose the elements you want from the different galleries.

Themes and styles also help keep your document coordinated. When you click Design and choose a new Theme, the pictures, charts, and SmartArt graphics change to match your new theme. When you apply styles, your headings change to match the new theme.

Save time in Word with new buttons that show up where you need them. To change the way a picture fits in your document, click it and a button for layout options appears next to it. When you work on a table, click where you want to add a row or a column, and then click the plus sign.

Reading is easier, too, in the new Reading view. You can collapse parts of the document and focus on the text you want. If you need to stop reading before you reach the end, Word remembers where you left off - even on another device.

### Activity 3.5.2.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Activity 3.5.2.docx`

Pemberly Furniture and Interiors

Getting the Look you want

If you are intimidated by the prospect of decorating your home, you definitely are not alone. The endless choices available to today’s consumer can be overwhelming. To help you get the look you want, the designer s at PFI made the following suggestions.

### Ask Yourself Some Questions

Think about places you’ve been where you felt especially comfortable recently – a friend’s house, for instance, or a stylish restaurant. How did those places make you feel? What did you like about them? Ask yourself the following questions about each place:

What was the colour scheme?

Was the room cluttered or bare?

Was the furniture made of wood or metal, or was it upholstered?

Would you describe the room as formal or casual?

For the next weeks, continue to analyse your surroundings whenever you visit a new place. Then, with this new awareness of your likes and dislikes, make a list of elements you want to incorporate into your own home. It’s also helpful to make a list of elements you want to avoid.

### Pick a style

Now that you know what you like and don’t like, you are ready to identify the style that suits your taste. Having an official name for your style will help sales people zero in on the products that will work best for you. People who are seriously interested in interior decorating make fine distinctions between styles. French country, cottage style, vintage retro, and Victorian are only some of the many possibilities. However, if your goal is simply to give your house a pleasant, coherent look, there’s no need to go into such detail. Most likely, your tastes fall into one of four basic styles:

Formal

Traditional

Eclectic

Contemporary

Stay true to your style

People who enjoy spending time thinking about and decorating their homes will often mix-and-match furniture and accessories from thesefour basic styles. You can pick a style for your house and stick with it. Note that you probably will want to combine styles within your house (for example, a formal living room and a casual family room). Within an individual room, however, a single style is the surest way to create a pleasing interior.

Of course, you probably won’t be able to redo every room in your house from top to bottom according to your chosen styles. For most people, decorating a home involves a series of purchases and improvement over time.

To maximize your design statement, consider making your purchases in the following order:

Large items such as sofas, beds, and dining tables

Lamps or other accent pieces

End tables and other accent pieces

Rugs or other floor coverings

While you are saving money to complete the look of a particular room, purchase smaller items (vases, mirrors, or picture frames) that can accentuate a room’s style without breaking your budget.

These are just some ideas to help you get started. To learn more about decorating your home, consider scheduling a consultation with Natalie Lanci, or one of are other fine designers at PFI

Getting Started With Our Staff

Pemberly employs four interior designers and five interior decorators. This award winning team is available to help you conceive, plan, and create an interior that will give you lasting pleasure.

Interior designers are responsible for planning the physical structure of a space. They control all technical details, such as structural requirements, health and safety issues and building codes. All interior designers at Pemberly have passed the NCID certification exam and are state licensed.

Peter Hernandez: Residential kitchen and bathroom specialist, graduate of the California Art Institute

Casey Rikli: Outdoor living specialist, graduate of the University of California, Davis Interior Decorators

There is no state wide accreditation program for interior decorators, but each of Pemberly’s interior decorators has at least ten years of residential and commercial experience.

The following list introduces our decorators and their specialities:

Getting Started Step by Step

Are you contemplating a major renovation of your current home? You may be wondering exactly how Pemberly Furniture and interiors can help. For most people, the process goes like this:

Schedule a free, forty-five minute consultation with one of our interior designers to discuss possible alterations to your structure (such as removing walls or adding rooms).

Interview potential construction firms, using your design plan as the basis of all construction bids.

Review the preliminary design plan and return comments to your designer.

Review the final design plan.

Schedule a free, forty-five minute consultation with one of our interior decorators to discuss the lighting, wall colours and overall appearance of your new room.

Proceed with construction, conferring as necessary with your new room, in construction with your Pemberly interior decorator.

Purchase appropriate furniture, carpets and lighting for your new room, in consultation with your Pemberly interior decorator.

The Hiker’s Credo

Hiker's Responsibility & Code of Ethics

The Hiker’s Credo

It is our privilege & not our right to enjoy the bountifulness of what nature has to offer. Nobody owns nature & by using this natural resource we build up a debt that can only be paid by using every means that we can to understand, protect & nurture what we have inherited.

Hiker’s Responsibility & Code of Ethics

1/.Never damage property of any kind –

a)fences,

b)signs and

c)buildings.

2/. Leave all gates as found & only deviate from the marked trail should an emergency dictate

3/. Build fires only in designated areas & then only when it is considered safe to do so. Hiker’s gas stoves must also only be used in an area clear of combustible material.

4/.Do not litter.

4.1All alien material must be carried along until one finds a suitable rubbish bin at an overnight camp or at the end of the trail.

4.2Additional care must be taken by smokers that ash is not the cause of a veld fire. A suitable enclosed receptacle should be used for this purpose.

4.3While Banana peels & apple cores might be considered as ‘biologically degradable’ these should also be carried out & disposed of in a rubbish bin.

5/. Do not write names or make markings on rocks or carve on trees. Do not touch or breathe directly on San paintings.

6/. Do not pick any plants or disturb any wild animals or their habitat

7/. Never hike alone. Take at least two companions with you. Also take cognizance of the possibility of crime in an area that you propose to hike in.

Dolphin Trail 3 Eastern Cape

### Article on Spekboom.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Article on Spekboom.docx`

Portulacaria afra

Portulacaria afra (known as elephant bush, dwarf jade plant, porkbush and spekboom in Afrikaans) is a small-leaved succulent plant found in South Africa. These succulents commonly have a reddish stem and leaves that are green, but also a variegated cultivar is often seen in cultivation. They are simple to care for and make easy houseplants for a sunny location. In frost-free regions they may be used in outdoor landscaping. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaans) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Succulent_plant) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa)

Description

It is a soft-wooded, semi-evergreen upright shrub or small tree, usually 2.5–4.5 metres (8–15 ft) tall. Similar in appearance to the unrelated "jade plant" Crassula ovata (family Crassulaceae), P. afra has smaller and rounder pads and more compact growth (shorter internodal spaces, down to 1.5 mm). It is much hardier, faster growing, more loosely branched, and has more limber tapering branches than Crassula once established.[1] (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crassula_ovata) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crassulaceae) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portulacaria_afra)

The genus Portulacaria has been shown to be an outlier, relatively unrelated to the other genera in the family, which are all restricted to small ranges in the arid far west of Southern Africa. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portulacaria) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Africa)

Distribution and habitat

It is very widespread in the east of South Africa (including Eswatini). In this moist climate, it is relatively rare, and tends to favour dryer rocky outcrops and slopes. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eswatini)

It is also found in much denser numbers in the dryer southern Cape. Here it occurs from the Little Karoo of the Western Cape, eastwards up until the thicket vegetation of the Eastern Cape. Spekboom is found most prolifically in the Albany thickets, a woodland ecoregion, which locally is often called noorsveld, after the high number of succulent Euphorbia species, which are often called noors plants. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Karoo) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thicket) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Cape) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albany_thickets) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregion) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphorbia)

Cultivation and uses

"Prostrata" decumbent variety

In the wilds of South Africa, large plants do survive the winter frosts by growing dense enough to provide their own natural cover. Drought-tolerant and fire-resistant, it will endure desert sun and heat once established, which the jade plant will not. Portulacaria is a common landscape plant in Phoenix, Arizona. Cuttings root very easily in most potting media. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuttings_(plant))

### Minecraft.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Minecraft.docx`

Minecraft is a sandbox video game created by Swedish developer Markus Persson, released by Mojang in 2011 and purchased by Microsoft in 2014. It is the single best-selling video game of all time, selling over 180 million copies across all platforms by late 2019, with over 112 million monthly active players. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandbox_video_game) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_game_developer) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Persson) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mojang) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best-selling_video_game)

In Minecraft, playrs explore an intentionally blocky, pixelated, procedurally-generated 3D world, and may discover and extract raw materials, craft tools, build structures or earthworks, and, depending on game mode, can fight video-controlled foes, as well as either cooperate with or compete against other players in the same world. These modes include a survival mode, in which players must acquire resources to build the world and maintin health, and a creative mode, where players have unlimited resources. In the Java Edition, players can modify the game with mods to create new gameplay mechanics, items, textures and assets (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixelization) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procedurally-generated) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3D_computer_graphics) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crafting_(gaming)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthworks_(engineering)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mob_(gaming)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survival_mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java_(software_platform)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_mods)

Minecraft is critically acclaimed, winning numerous awards, and has been described as one of the most influential and greatest video games of all time. Social medie, parodies, adaptations, merchandise, and the annual MineCon conventions played large roles in popularizing the game. It has also been used in educational environments, especially in the realm of computing systems, as virtual videos and hardwar devices have been built in it. A number of spin-off games have also been developed, such as Minecraft: Story Mode, Minecraft Earth, and Minecraft Dungeons. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_video_games_of_all_time) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MineCon) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft:_Story_Mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_Earth) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_Dungeons)

### Respect the Spekboom.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Respect the Spekboom.docx`

Respect the spekboom!

JULY 31ST, 2017

Portulacaria afra, commonly known as spekboom, is a drought-resistant, indigenous succulent native to our country. Although mainly an inhabitant of the Little Karoo and Eastern Cape, it also grows in dry river valleys in the east and up to the Limpopo, and on the slopes of the Soutpansberg. In late winter you may be lucky to see them covered in pink flowers. Here at Babylonstoren we have a nice spread of spekboom adjacent to the three fish ponds.

Huh? Spekboom eats my car’s carbon dioxide?

The spekboom earns enormous value for its carbon credits, and is highly regarded in terms of the voluntary carbon standard mechanism, making this humble oukie a red carpet rave on the international market. Although all plants trap carbon dioxide from the air to photosynthesize and grow, spekboom is highly efficient at this because it can still trap carbon dioxide even if its breathing pores are closed. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voluntary_Carbon_Standard)

Here’s how it works: Spekboom has the ability to change its photosynthesis pathway depending on rainfall and temperature. When conditions are favourable, it trots the same pathway as other plants (C3 photosynthesis). However, during harshly dry conditions, it switches to a different type of photosynthesis called CAM (Crassulacean Acid Metabolism). Now the plant closes the pores (or stoma) in its leaves and branches during the day so that no unnecessary transpiration takes place. Yet it remains able to photosynthesise. This is made possible by acid that builds up in the leaves during the night (stored as carbon dioxide), and is broken down during the day. In this way our friend efficiently captures large amounts of carbon using very little water.

### Spekboom salad.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Spekboom salad.docx`

Spekboom, Chickpea And Tomato Salad

by Jacques Erasmus for Woolworths Taste

Ingredients

2 x 400 g cans chickpeas, drained and rinsed

250 g baby tomatoes

2 avocados, peeled, pitted and sliced

1/2 onion, sliced and blanched

two handfuls spekboom leaves, rinsed

Maldon salt, to taste

For the dressing, mix together

1/3 cup extra virgin olive oil

3 T white-wine vinegar

1 T honey

1 T smooth mustard

freshly ground black pepper

2 sprigs flat-leaf parsley, roughly chopped

Method

Remove the chickpea shells and place in a salad bowl with the tomato, avocado, onion and Spekboom leaves and pour over the dressing. Lightly mix and allow to rest for 2 to 3 minutes before serving, sprinkled with salt, to taste.

### Spekboom.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Spekboom.docx`

boldStyles: Intense Quote21 MARCH 2019 | PUBLISHED BY ADMIN

Styles: Book TitleSpekboom (Portulacaria Afra) is a succulent plant found in South Africa. It is indigenous to the Eastern Cape province and has been deemed as a miracle plant by many. It is a bright green, small-leaved plant with a contrasting red stem found in Southern Africa that seems ordinary but don’t be fooled – it is a very special plant!

Interesting Facts About the Spekboom Plant & Its Uses

italicunderlineThe spekboom plant is a sprawling shrub or small tree; occurring in karroid (semi desert) areas and bushveld. It is usually found in rocky places and grows prolifically in parts of the Eastern Cape. The leaves are circular and bright green or pale grey. The plant has a glossy red-brown trunk and bears a dense crown of succulent leaves and stems.

Styles: Subtle EmphasisIt is most attractive in full bloom as a mass of soft pink nectar-rich flowers flourish at the ends of the branchlets. After flowering, tiny papery three-winged fruits populate the unassuming spekboom plant. This versatile plant has several uses:

#1 Leaves are Edible

The taste of spekboom leaves are pleasant but changes throughout as the sun rises and sets. During the day leaves have an acid flavour and they become less acidic towards the evening. The delicious greenery is heavily browsed by game and firm favourite of several wild animals, especially elephants!

#2 Communities Use Spekboom Leaves for Their Medicinal Values

The leaves are used medicinally and in traditional home construction. In certain areas, the stems are used to help build huts/homes. The stems are dried and used as thatch for rooves of the huts/homes. The spekboom flowers are nectar-rich and provide food for many insects – endangered bees love them! This, in turn, attract insectivorous birds. Larvae of the Duadem butterfly also feed on the Portulacaria species.

Font: Broadway, 14ptFont color: Purple

### The History of animation.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/The History of animation.docx`

The History of animation
Animation is the capturing of sequential, static images—drawings or photos of inanimate objects—and playing them in rapid succession to mimic real world motion. If you’ve ever seen a flip book, you know how it works.

But chances are, when you ask “what is animation?” you want something more than a technical definition. Where did it come from? What’s the appeal?

Although the idea of sequential art dates back thousands of years—more, depending on your interpretation of cave paintings—animation as we know it was only made possible by the invention of the film strip. However, the optics behind animation has been known since the early 1800s, as we see from Simon von Stampfer’s phénakisticope(1833) below.

On of the first animation on film was actually hand-painted by the French artist and inventor Charles-Emile Reynaud on his Praxinoscope—one of the stepping-stone inventions that led to the film camera.

For the first part of the 1900s, artists from all over the world experimented with animation, such as the recently discovered Katsudō Shashin, dated from 1907 to 1911 and said to predate much of Western animation. However, animation was thought of mostly as a novelty until the era of Walt Disney in the 1920s, when sound was beginning to be implemented in movies. Steamboat Willie (1928), Disney’s “flagship” cartoon, was not the first cartoon with sound, but it was one of the most popular at the time. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katsud%C5%8D_Shashin)

By the 1930s, other studios caught on that there was money to be made through animation, the impetus behind Warner Bros. launching Looney Tunes and Merrie Melodies. The 1930s saw friendly rivalry between Mickey and Bugs — known now as the American Golden Age of Animation — which culminated in a Disney’s power play Snow White and the Seven Dwarves, the first feature-length film to be made entirely of hand-drawn images.

The rest is history. With the popularization of color TV in the late 1950s, another American animation pioneer Hanna-Barbera entered the scene. Following the 1960s cultural revolution, animation was co-opted for adults, most famously the X-rated Fritz the Cat (1972) based on the work of legendary comic artist Robert Crumb. Finally, in the 1990s, computer generated imagery (CGI) was able to compete with hand-drawn animation, popularized by the first completely-CGI film Toy Story (1995).

Considering the wide-ranging history of animation, we see its appeal is wide-ranging as well. Kids love it, of course, because most of the stories for children involve talking animals, tea kettles, or visuals otherwise impossible to bring to life.

But the more sophisticated the animations techniques become, so too do the themes they discuss. While CGI is used in almost every movie today, even completely animated features can appeal to adults—just look at popular animated sitcoms like The Simpsons, Family Guy or South Park.

Perhaps the greatest strength isn’t that it appeals to different groups, but all groups at once. Animation allows artists to invoke that childlike wonder and awe of not believing our eyes, but to harness that power for more mature storytelling and even business, as we explain below.

How can this visual technique appeal to both our inner child and our discerning adult at the same time? Just look at practically any Pixar movie. This is a safe space—are you going to tell me you didn’t get misty-eyed during the first ten minutes of Up?

### What is animation.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/What is animation.docx`

What is animation: a guide to its history and modern use in design?

There was a point in everyone’s life when animation was just the coolest thing you’d ever seen. Whether it was a Disney movie as a kid or maybe a 3D game cut scene that blew you away, there was always something spectacular when imaginary characters move and act as in real life.

And don’t think that power went unnoticed by graphic designers.

In the digital era, animation can do more than just entertain children with a boat-driving mouse. It’s an effective tool for visual communication. Of course, it offers a whole new medium for expression and creativity, but on a more practical level, the movement of animation attracts more attention than static images. And considering the restraints of live footage, sometimes an animation of a bouncing ball works best.

In this article, we give an all-encompassing animation overview. We discuss animation for designers and give some techniques from the heyday of cartoons a much-needed update for the modern era. We also showcase the business side of animation, explaining how it can improve both the UX of your website and the effectiveness of digital marketing campaigns. So without further ado, let’s get moving!

### Sports_Day_Gr8.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Practical Project/Question 1/Sports_Day_Gr8.docx`

Sports Day Activities for Secondary School

Sports dy is the highlight of the school year for lots of pupils and teachers, so you want to make it an event to remember with lots of activities to enjoy. There are a few things to consider when planning a secondary school sports dy; there are usually far more children in Secondary school than in Primary, and there's a great age difference. Some schools plan to have 2 sports dys, 1 for Years 7-9 and the other for Years 10-11, whilst others choose to wait until after the Year 11 pupils have left the school in the summer term and host a whole school event.

Who's Involved?

Is it best to involve everyone, from children to parents, or keep it as a school-only affair?

For Primary schools, the school sports dy is usually the highlight of the summer for parents as well as the kids, so the parents love to be involved and join in as much as possible. They're quite happy to join in the races and help on the dy.

Once the children move to Secondary school, the kids are quite happy to take part and help with arranging the events independently of their parents, so many Secondary schools host the event during school hours just for the children.

Choosing the Events

Do you choose some elements of the Primary games such Egg and Spoon races and three-legged games, or do you stick with track events and the more adventurous sports activities the children use in Secondary School?

Here are some traditional sports dy event ideas:

100m Sprint Race

Use your school running track for one race per year group.

800m Race

Use your school running track for one race per year group.

Relay Race

1 race per year group taking part.

Using your school running track, give your teams a coloured bib and a matching relay baton.

Set out the crossover areas on your school running track.

Discus

Using your school playing field, set out the area safely away from other passing students.

Using a long line tape measure, measure the distance thrown for each pupil taking part.

Shot Put

Using your school playing field, set out the area safely away from other passing students.

Using a long line tape measure, measure the distance thrown for each pupil taking part.

Javelin

Using your school playing field, set out the area safely away from other passing students.

Using a long line tape measure, measure the distance thrown per each pupil taking part.

High Jump

One event per year group

Usually set up as an indoor event with crash mats.

Use your school high jump equipment.

Tug of War

1 team per year group

Teachers Race

Challenge the teachers to a race of their own. Which teacher is fastest, the PE teacher or perhaps a sporty teacher from a different department?

Challenging Games for Everyone

Getting all children involved in the sports dy can be tricky and you usually find that some children in the class are a bit more reserved when it comes to sport.

To make sure they're still active and enjoying the dy, include a few challenging games that aren't quite as 'sporty' but just as competitive and exciting:

Limbo

Pick up a limbo set and set the challenge for the most flexible students.

Reverse Basketball Throw

Challenge your best netball and basketball players that little bit further; set the rules so they must score goals by throwing the ball backwards over their heads!

Football Goal kick

Get the PE teacher in the net and see how many goals you can get passed them; highest score wins.

Table Tennis

Do you have budding table tennis players ready for a challenge?

Traditional primary games of three-legged race and the egg and spoon race are more likely to attract the children less keen on sport as they see this as a more fun event than a typical racing event. By having some of these games included, you have more opportunity for inclusion of the whole school.

The following need to be consider for the sports dy:

- Sun protection against sunburn

- Plenty of water not to dehydrate

- Prevention against heat stroke or heat exhaustion

- Warm up properly

Are you ready for your school sport’s dy? Across the country, children will be lining up hoping to win the 100 metres dash, the sack race or the egg and spoon race. More importantly, many enthusiastic and competitive mums and dads will be hoping to excel in the parents race too.

Please complete the following survey below to indicate your interest in a game, and if you are willing to participate in our sports dy:

| Sport Dy Survey |  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Name & Surname |  | Childs Name & Surname:<br> | Childs Grade: |
| Please indicate the THREE EVENTs that you are interested in: |  |  |  |
| Event 1 | Event 2 | Event 3 |  |
|  |  |  |  |
| Please indicate if you will be interested in the parent's race | Yes | No |  |

Details of the sport dy are as follows:

Date: Saturdy, 17th November

Time: 8:00 – 15:00

### Activity 3.2.2.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Activity 3.2.2.docx`

21 MARCH 2019 | PUBLISHED BY ADMIN

Spekboom (Portulacaria Afra) is a succulent plant found in South Africa. It is indigenous to the Eastern Cape province and has been deemed as a miracle plant by many. It is a bright green, small-leaved plant with a contrasting red stem found in Southern Africa that seems ordinary but don’t be fooled – it is a very special plant!

Interesting Facts About the Spekboom Plant & Its Uses

The spekboom plant is a sprawling shrub or small tree; occurring in karroid (semi desert) areas and bushveld. It is usually found in rocky places and grows prolifically in parts of the Eastern Cape. The leaves are circular and bright green or pale grey. The plant has a glossy red-brown trunk and bears a dense crown of succulent leaves and stems.

It is most attractive in full bloom as a mass of soft pink nectar-rich flowers flourish at the ends of the branchlets. After flowering, tiny papery three-winged fruits populate the unassuming spekboom plant. This versatile plant has several uses:

#1 Leaves are Edible

The taste of spekboom leaves are pleasant but changes throughout as the sun rises and sets. During the day leaves have an acid flavour and they become less acidic towards the evening. The delicious greenery is heavily browsed by game and firm favourite of several wild animals, especially elephants!

#2 Communities Use Spekboom Leaves for Their Medicinal Values

The leaves are used medicinally and in traditional home construction. In certain areas, the stems are used to help build huts/homes. The stems are dried and used as thatch for rooves of the huts/homes. The spekboom flowers are nectar-rich and provide food for many insects – endangered bees love them! This, in turn, attract insectivorous birds. Larvae of the Duadem butterfly also feed on the Portulacaria species.

Minecraft 
Introduction
Minecraft is a sandbox computer game created by Swedish developer Markus Persson, released by Mojang in 2011 and purchased by Microsoft in 2014. It is the single best-selling computer game of all time, selling over 180 million copies across all platforms by late 2019, with over 112 million monthly active gamers. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandbox_video_game) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Video_game_developer) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Markus_Persson) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mojang) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microsoft) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Best-selling_video_game)

Game environment
In Minecraft, gamers explore an intentionally blocky, pixelated, procedurally-generated 3D world, and may discover and extract raw materials, craft tools, build structures or earthworks, and, depending on game mode, can fight computer-controlled foes, as well as either cooperate with or compete against other gamers in the same world. These modes include a survival mode, in which gamers must acquire resources to build the world and maintain health, and a creative mode, where gamers have unlimited resources. In the Java Edition, gamers can modify the game with mods to create new gameplay mechanics, items, textures and assets (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixelization) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procedurally-generated) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3D_computer_graphics) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crafting_(gaming)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthworks_(engineering)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mob_(gaming)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survival_mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java_(software_platform)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_mods)

Greatest computer game
Minecraft is critically acclaimed, winning numerous awards, and has been described as one of the most influential and greatest computer games of all time. Social media, parodies, adaptations, merchandise, and the annual MineCon conventions played large roles in popularizing the game. It has also been used in educational environments, especially in the realm of computing systems, as virtual computers and hardware devices have been built in it. A number of spin-off games have also been developed, such as Minecraft: Story Mode, Minecraft Earth, and Minecraft Dungeons. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_video_games_of_all_time) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MineCon) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft:_Story_Mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_Earth) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_Dungeons)

### Activity 3.4.1.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Activity 3.4.1.docx`

Spekboom: Miracle plant and superfood

In case you were wondering, it doesn’t taste like bacon. Contrary to its colourful Afrikaans name, Spekboom (literally translated to ‘bacon tree’) is a lush, emerald succulent plant with juicy, round leaves and an unmistakable, lemony flavour. It grows naturally in the Western Cape and very prolifically in the Eastern Cape, where it earned its other name, ‘elephant bush’, due to its important place in the diet of elephants (the elephants of Addo Elephant Park have been known to eat up to 200kg of Spekboom per day!) Many years ago (according to the Cape Almanac of 1843), Spekboom was described as “one of the most valuable shrubs” around, due to the excellent food it provided for large flocks of sheep and goats, particularly in times of drought (ref). Today, many go as far as labeling it a “miracle plant” and “superfood”. I decided to find out why: (http:/www.spekboomfoundation.co.za/about.html)

**Visual fd5ffffa57f2a89c** (260 × 173 pixels)

It grows naturally in the Western Cape and very prolifically in the Eastern Cape, where it earned its other name, ‘elephant bush’, due to its important place in the diet of elephants (the elephants of Addo Elephant Park have been known to eat up to 200kg of Spekboom p

Image text: none detected. Do not infer exact labels or a solution from this description alone.

4 Qualities That Make Spekboom A ‘Superpower’ Plant:

It has enormous carbon-storing capabilities.

Simply put, Spekboom has the potential to mop up the excess CO2 responsible for climate change. Its immense carbon-storing capabilities and capacity to offset damaging carbon emissions are comparable to that of moist, subtropical forest.

It is fire-resistant and drought-resistant!

Spekboom doesn’t burn, making it a hardy plant to withstand veld fires and great material for firebreak hedges.

It ‘changes gears’ to adapt to drier climates and wetter climates.

While most plants require their stomata to be open during the daytime to absorb carbon dioxide, in dry conditions, the Spekboom can open its stomata at night instead, and close them again in the day to avoid loss of water. This slows down evaporation, and enables the Spekboom to grow faster during the day.

We can eat it!

Not only is Spekboom digestible by both domestic and wild herbivores – we can eat it too! Its little succulent leaves contain heaps of Vitamin C as well as a number of other minerals, and are best used in salads, my favourite of which is a spekboom, chickpea and tomato salad. Enjoy!

### Activity 3.5.2.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Activity 3.5.2.docx`

Pemberly Furniture and Interiors



**Visual 6c7806480ed4b015** (84 × 49 pixels)

Context-based description: small inline icon, symbol or interface detail accompanying the surrounding instruction.

Associated source passage: Pemberly Furniture and Interiors Getting the Look you want

Image text: none detected. Do not infer exact labels or a solution from this description alone.

Getting the Look you want

If you are intimidated by the prospect of decorating your home, you are not alone. The endless choices available to today’s consumer can be overwhelming. To help you get the look you want, the designer s at PEMBERLY FURNITURE AND INTERIORS made the following suggestions.

#### Ask Yourself Some Questions

Think about places you’ve been recently where you felt especially comfortable – a friend’s house, for instance, or a stylish restaurant. How did those places make you feel? What did you like about them? Ask yourself the following questions about each place:

What was the colour scheme?

Was the room cluttered or bare?

Was the furniture made of wood or metal, or was it upholstered?

Would you describe the room as formal or casual?

For the next weeks, continue to analyse your surroundings whenever you visit a new place. Then, with this new awareness of your likes and dislikes, make a list of elements you want to incorporate into your own home. It’s also helpful to make a list of elements you want to avoid.

#### Pick a style

Now that you know what you like and don’t like, you are ready to identify the style that suits your taste. Having an official name for your style will help sales people zero in on the products that will work best for you. People who are seriously interested in interior decorating make fine distinctions between styles. French country, cottage style, vintage retro, and Victorian are only some of the many possibilities. However, if your goal is simply to give your house a pleasant, coherent look, there’s no need to go into such detail. Most likely, your tastes fall into one of four basic styles:

Formal

Traditional

Eclectic

Contemporary

Stay true to your style

People who enjoy spending time thinking about and decorating their homes will often mix-and-match furniture and accessories from these four basic styles. You can pick a style for your house and stick with it. Note that you probably will want to combine styles within your house (for example, a formal living room and a casual family room). Within an individual room, however, a single style is the surest way to create a pleasing interior.

Of course, you probably won’t be able to redo every room in your house from top to bottom according to your chosen styles. For most people, decorating a home involves a series of purchases and improvement over time.

To maximize your design statement, consider making your purchases in the following order:

Large items such as sofas, beds, and dining tables

Lamps or other accent pieces

End tables and other accent pieces

Rugs or other floor coverings

While you are saving money to complete the look of a particular room, purchase smaller items (vases, mirrors, or picture frames) that can accentuate a room’s style without breaking your budget.

These are just some ideas to help you get started. To learn more about decorating your home, consider scheduling a consultation with Natalie Lanci, or one of are other fine designers at PEMBERLY FURNITURE AND INTERIORS

### Activity 3.6.1.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Activity 3.6.1.docx`

Major Renovations the Pemberly Way

Getting Started With Our Staff

Pemberly employs four interior designers and five interior decorators. This award winning team is available to help you conceive, plan, and create an interior that will give you lasting pleasure.

Interior designers are responsible for planning the physical structure of a space. They control all technical details, such as structural requirements, health and safety issues and building codes. All interior designers at Pemberly have passed the NCID certification exam and are state licensed.

Peter Hernandez: Residential kitchen and bathroom specialist, graduate of the California Art Institute

Katherine La Francois: Residential home specialist, winner of the 2006 Diego Contemporary Design Prize

Lynn O’Reilly: Commercial kitchen and dining specialist, lead designer for the Azure Table Inn and the subject of an Architecture Today profile in 2005

Casey Rikli: Outdoor living specialist, graduate of the University of California, Davis Interior Decorators

Interior decorators help you select colour schemes, layout, furniture, lighting, floor coverings, and window treatments. There is no state wide accreditation program for interior decorators, but each of Pemberly’s interior decorators has at least ten years of residential and commercial experience.

The following list introduces our decorators and their specialities:

Jonathan Ivory: Formal commercial and residential interiors, 18th century antiques, Persian rugs

Ernesto Livorni: Eclectic residential interiors, mission styles furniture, William Morris fabric, and wall coverings

Kaila Peterson: Traditional residential interiors, 19th century quilts, colonial fabric arts

Mai YangXiong: Contemporary commercial and residential interiors

Julia Brock: Feng Shui, environmentally friendly design

GETTING STARTED STEP BY STEP

Are you contemplating a major renovation of your current home? You may be wondering exactly how Pemberly Furniture and interiors can help. For most people, the process goes like this:

Schedule a free, forty-five minute consultation with one of our interior designers to discuss possible alterations to your structure (such as removing walls or adding rooms).

Schedule a two hour on-site consultation with an interior designer at your home.

Review the preliminary design plan and return comments to your designer.

Review the final design plan. Interview potential construction firms, using your design plan as the basis of all construction bids.

Schedule a free, forty-five minute consultation with one of our interior decorators to discuss the lighting, wall colours and overall appearance of your new room.

Proceed with construction, conferring as necessary with your new room, in construction with your Pemberly interior decorator.

Purchase appropriate furniture, carpets and lighting for your new room, in consultation with your Pemberly interior decorator.

Enjoy your beautiful knew interior.

Are you ready to get started? Call today to schedule your free, forty-five minute design construction. Better living in your well-designed home is only a phone call away.

Call: Monica Peters 0823333456

### Activity 3.7.1.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Activity 3.7.1.docx`

The Hiker’s Credo

Hiker's Responsibility and Code of Ethics

The Hiker’s Credo

It is our privilege and not our right to enjoy the bountifulness of what nature has to offer. Nobody owns nature and by using this natural resource we build up a debt that can only be paid by using every means that we can to understand, protect and nurture what we have inherited.

Hiker’s Responsibility and Code of Ethics

1) Never damage property of any kind –

- fences,

- signs and

- buildings.

2) Leave all gates as found and only deviate from the marked trail should an emergency dictate

3) Build fires only in designated areas and then only when it is considered safe to do so. Hiker’s gas stoves must also only be used in an area clear of combustible material.

4) Do not litter.

- All alien material must be carried along until one finds a suitable rubbish bin at an overnight camp or at the end of the trail.

- Additional care must be taken by smokers that ash is not the cause of a veld fire. A suitable enclosed receptacle should be used for this purpose.

- While Banana peels and apple cores might be considered as ‘biologically degradable’ these should also be carried out and disposed of in a rubbish bin.

5) Do not write names or make markings on rocks or carve on trees. Do not touch or breathe directly on San paintings.

6) Do not pick any plants or disturb any wild animals or their habitat

7) Never hike alone. Take at least two companions with you. Also take cognizance of the possibility of crime in an area that you propose to hike in.

Long Distance Trails (Three days or more)

Trail Name Numbers of Days Region

Amatola Trail 6 Eastern Cape

Dolphin Trail 3 Eastern Cape

Emoveni Trail 5 KwaZulu Natal

Giants Cup Trail 5 KwaZulu Natal

Kilimanjaro 6 Tanzania

### Article on Spekboom.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Article on Spekboom.docx`

Portulacaria afra

Portulacaria afra (known as elephant bush, dwarf jade plant, porkbush and spekboom in Afrikaans) is a small-leaved succulent plant found in South Africa. These succulents commonly have a reddish stem and leaves that are green, but also a variegated cultivar is often seen in cultivation. They are simple to care for and make easy houseplants for a sunny location. In frost-free regions they may be used in outdoor landscaping. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Afrikaans) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Succulent_plant) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa)

#### Description

It is a soft-wooded, semi-evergreen upright shrub or small tree, usually 2.5–4.5 metres (8–15 ft) tall. Similar in appearance to the unrelated "jade plant" Crassula ovata (family Crassulaceae), P. afra has smaller and rounder pads and more compact growth (shorter internodal spaces, down to 1.5 mm). It is much hardier, faster growing, more loosely branched, and has more limber tapering branches than Crassula once established.[1] (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crassula_ovata) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crassulaceae) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portulacaria_afra)

The genus Portulacaria has been shown to be an outlier, relatively unrelated to the other genera in the family, which are all restricted to small ranges in the arid far west of Southern Africa. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portulacaria) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Africa)

#### Distribution and habitat

It is very widespread in the east of South Africa (including Eswatini). In this moist climate, it is relatively rare, and tends to favour dryer rocky outcrops and slopes. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Africa) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eswatini)

It is also found in much denser numbers in the dryer southern Cape. Here it occurs from the Little Karoo of the Western Cape, eastwards up until the thicket vegetation of the Eastern Cape. Spekboom is found most prolifically in the Albany thickets, a woodland ecoregion, which locally is often called noorsveld, after the high number of succulent Euphorbia species, which are often called noors plants. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Karoo) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_Cape) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thicket) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Cape) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albany_thickets) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecoregion) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphorbia)

#### Cultivation and uses

"Prostrata" decumbent variety

In the wilds of South Africa, large plants do survive the winter frosts by growing dense enough to provide their own natural cover. Drought-tolerant and fire-resistant, it will endure desert sun and heat once established, which the jade plant will not. Portulacaria is a common landscape plant in Phoenix, Arizona. Cuttings root very easily in most potting media. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuttings_(plant))

In Minecraft, players explore an intentionally blocky, pixelated, procedurally-generated 3D world, and may discover and extract raw materials, craft tools, build structures or earthworks, and, depending on game mode, can fight computer-controlled foes, as well as either cooperate with or compete against other players in the same world. These modes include a survival mode, in which players must acquire resources to build the world and maintain health, and a creative mode, where players have unlimited resources. In the Java Edition, players can modify the game with mods to create new gameplay mechanics, items, textures and assets (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pixelization) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Procedurally-generated) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3D_computer_graphics) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crafting_(gaming)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthworks_(engineering)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Game_mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mob_(gaming)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survival_mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Java_(software_platform)) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_mods)

Minecraft is critically acclaimed, winning numerous awards, and has been described as one of the most influential and greatest computer games of all time. Social media, parodies, adaptations, merchandise, and the annual MineCon conventions played large roles in popularizing the game. It has also been used in educational environments, especially in the realm of computing systems, as virtual computers and hardware devices have been built in it. A number of spin-off games have also been developed, such as Minecraft: Story Mode, Minecraft Earth, and Minecraft Dungeons. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greatest_video_games_of_all_time) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MineCon) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft:_Story_Mode) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_Earth) (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minecraft_Dungeons)

### Activity 3.1.2.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Practical assignment_Your name/Activity 3.1.2.docx`

Name and Surname
Spekboom is edible, with a very high nutritional value. It is a favoured food of black rhinos, elephants and kudus. The good news is that we can eat it too. With a slightly lemony taste, Spekboom leaves are juicy and full of moisture, making them the perfect 'pick-me-up' during a long day's hiking in the arid Karoo.

### Respect the Spekboom.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/Respect the Spekboom.docx`

Respect the spekboom spekboom!

Portulacaria afra, commonly known as spekboom, is a drought-resistant, indigenous succulent native to our country. Although mainly an inhabitant of the Little Karoo and Eastern Cape, it also grows in dry river valleys in the east and up to the Limpopo, and on the slopes of the Soutpansberg. In late winter you may be lucky to see them covered in pink flowers. Here at Babylonstoren we have a nice spread of spekboom adjacent to the three fish ponds.

Huh? Spekboom eats my car’s carbon dioxide?

The spekboom earns enormous value for its carbon credits, and is highly regarded in terms of the voluntary carbon standard mechanism, making this humble oukie a red carpet rave on the international market. Although all plants trap carbon dioxide from the air to photosynthesize and grow, spekboom is highly efficient at this because it can still trap carbon dioxide even if its breathing pores are closed. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voluntary_Carbon_Standard)

Here’s how it works: Spekboom has the ability to change its photosynthesis pathway depending on rainfall and temperature. When conditions are favourable, it trots the same pathway as other plants (C3 photosynthesis). However, during harshly dry conditions, it switches to a different type of photosynthesis called CAM (Crassulacean Acid Metabolism). Now the plant closes the pores (or stoma) in its leaves and branches during the day so that no unnecessary transpiration takes place. Yet it remains able to photosynthesise. This is made possible by acid that builds up in the leaves during the night (stored as carbon dioxide), and is broken down during the day. In this way our friend efficiently captures large amounts of carbon using very little water.

JULY 31ST, 2017

### The History of animation.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/The History of animation.docx`

The History of animation
Animation is the capturing of sequential, static images—drawings or photos of inanimate objects—and playing them in rapid succession to mimic real world motion. If you’ve ever seen a flip book, you know how it works.

But chances are, when you ask “what is animation?” you want something more than a technical definition. Where did it come from? What’s the appeal?

Although the idea of sequential art dates back thousands of years—more, depending on your interpretation of cave paintings—animation as we know it was only made possible by the invention of the film strip. However, the optics behind animation has been known since the early 1800s, as we see from Simon von Stampfer’s phénakisticope(1833) below.

On of the first animation on film was actually hand-painted by the French artist and inventor Charles-Émile Reynaud on his Praxinoscope—one of the stepping-stone inventions that led to the film camera.

For the first part of the 1900s, artists from all over the world experimented with animation, such as the recently discovered Katsudō Shashin, dated from 1907 to 1911 and said to predate much of Western animation. However, animation was thought of mostly as a novelty until the era of Walt Disney in the 1920s, when sound was beginning to be implemented in movies. Steamboat Willie (1928), Disney’s “flagship” cartoon, was not the first cartoon with sound, but it was one of the most popular at the time. (https:/en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katsud%C5%8D_Shashin)

By the 1930s, other studios caught on that there was money to be made through animation, the impetus behind Warner Bros. launching Looney Tunes and Merrie Melodies. The 1930s saw friendly rivalry between Mickey and Bugs — known now as the American Golden Age of Animation — which culminated in a Disney’s power play Snow White and the Seven Dwarves, the first feature-length film to be made entirely of hand-drawn images.

The rest is history. With the popularization of color TV in the late 1950s, another American animation pioneer Hanna-Barbera entered the scene. Following the 1960s cultural revolution, animation was co-opted for adults, most famously the X-rated Fritz the Cat (1972) based on the work of legendary comic artist Robert Crumb. Finally, in the 1990s, computer generated imagery (CGI) was able to compete with hand-drawn animation, popularized by the first completely-CGI film Toy Story (1995).

Considering the wide-ranging history of animation, we see its appeal is wide-ranging as well. Kids love it, of course, because most of the stories for children involve talking animals, tea kettles, or visuals otherwise impossible to bring to life.

But the more sophisticated the animations techniques become, so too do the themes they discuss. While CGI is used in almost every movie today, even completely animated features can appeal to adults—just look at popular animated sitcoms like The Simpsons, Family Guy or South Park.

Perhaps the greatest strength isn’t that it appeals to different groups, but all groups at once. Animation allows artists to invoke that childlike wonder and awe of not believing our eyes, but to harness that power for more mature storytelling and even business, as we explain below.

How can this visual technique appeal to both our inner child and our discerning adult at the same time? Just look at practically any Pixar movie. This is a safe space—are you going to tell me you didn’t get misty-eyed during the first ten minutes of Up?

### What is animation.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Chapter 3 Word Solution Files/What is animation.docx`

What is animation: a guide to its history and modern use in design?

There was a point in everyone’s life when animation was just the coolest thing you’d ever seen. Whether it was a Disney movie as a kid or maybe a 3D game cut scene that blew you away, there was always something spectacular when imaginary characters move and act as in real life. And don’t think that power went unnoticed by graphic designers.

In the digital era, animation can do more than just entertain children with a boat-driving mouse. It’s an effective tool for visual communication. Of course, it offers a whole new medium for expression and creativity, but on a more practical level, the movement of animation attracts more attention than static images. And considering the restraints of live footage, sometimes an animation of a bouncing ball works best.

In this article, we give an all-encompassing animation overview. We discuss animation for designers and give some techniques from the heyday of cartoons a much-needed update for the modern era. We also showcase the business side of animation, explaining how it can improve both the UX of your website and the effectiveness of digital marketing campaigns. So without further ado, let’s get moving!

### Sol_Sports_Day_Gr8.docx

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Solutions/Practical Project.zip!/Practical Project/Question 1/Sol_Sports_Day_Gr8.docx`

Sports Day Activities for Secondary School

Sports day is the highlight of the school year for lots of pupils and teachers, so you want to make it an event to remember with lots of activities to enjoy. There are a few things to consider when planning a secondary school sports day; there are usually far more children in Secondary school than in Primary, and there's a great age difference. Some schools plan to have 2 sports days, 1 for Years 7-9 and the other for Years 10-11, whilst others choose to wait until after the Year 11 pupils have left the school in the summer term and host a whole school event.

Who's Involved?

Is it best to involve everyone, from children to parents, or keep it as a school-only affair?

For Primary schools, the school sports day is usually the highlight of the summer for parents as well as the kids, so the parents love to be involved and join in as much as possible. They're quite happy to join in the races and help on the day.

Once the children move to Secondary school, the kids are quite happy to take part and help with arranging the events independently of their parents, so many Secondary schools host the event during school hours just for the children.

Choosing the Events

Do you choose some elements of the Primary games such Egg and Spoon races and three-legged games, or do you stick with track events and the more adventurous sports activities the children use in Secondary School?

Here are some traditional sports day event ideas;

1. 100m Sprint Race

Use your school running track for one race per year group.

2. 800m Race

Use your school running track for one race per year group.

3. Relay Race

1 race per year group taking part.

Using your school running track, give your teams a coloured bib and a matching relay baton.

Set out the crossover areas on your school running track.

4. Discus

Using your school playing field, set out the area safely away from other passing students.

Using a long line tape measure, measure the distance thrown for each pupil taking part.

5. Shot Put

Using your school playing field, set out the area safely away from other passing students.

Using a long line tape measure, measure the distance thrown for each pupil taking part.

6. Javelin

Using your school playing field, set out the area safely away from other passing students.

Using a long line tape measure, measure the distance thrown per each pupil taking part.

7. High Jump

One event per year group

Usually set up as an indoor event with crash mats.

Use your school high jump equipment.

8. Tug of War

1 team per year group

9. Teachers Race

Challenge the teachers to a race of their own. Which teacher is fastest; the PE teacher or perhaps a sporty teacher from a different department?

Challenging Games for Everyone

Getting all children involved in the sports day can be tricky and you usually find that some children in the class are a bit more reserved when it comes to sport.

To make sure they're still active and enjoying the day, include a few challenging games that aren't quite as 'sporty' but just as competitive and exciting:

Limbo

Pick up a limbo set and set the challenge for the most flexible students.

Reverse Basketball Throw

Challenge your best netball and basketball players that little bit further; set the rules so they must score goals by throwing the ball backwards over their heads!

Football Goal kick

Get the PE teacher in the net and see how many goals you can get passed them; highest score wins.

Table Tennis

Do you have budding table tennis players ready for a challenge?

Traditional primary games of three-legged race and the egg and spoon race are more likely to attract the children less keen on sport as they see this as a more fun event than a typical racing event. By having some of these games included, you have more opportunity for inclusion of the whole school.

The following need to be consider for the sports day:

- Sun protection against sunburn

- Plenty of water not to dehydrate

- Prevention against heat stroke or heat exhaustion

- Warm up properly

Are you ready for your school sport’s day? Across the country, children will be lining up hoping to win the 100 metres dash, the sack race or the egg and spoon race. More importantly, many enthusiastic and competitive mums and dads will be hoping to excel in the parents race too.

Please complete the following survey below to indicate your interest in a game, and if you are willing to participate in our sports day:

### Dolphin.jpg

### Dolphins.doc

Source: `9. TEACHER GUIDE/Grade 8/Datafiles/Chapter 3 Word Data Files/Dolphins.doc`

Legacy Word text transcription. Original visual formatting is not retained.

Dolphins are  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_mammal" \o "Marine mammal" marine mammals closely related to  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whale" \o "Whale" whales and  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Porpoise" \o "Porpoise" porpoises. There are almost forty species of dolphin in 17  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genus" \o "Genus" genera. They vary in size from 1.2 m (4 ft) and 40 kg (90 lb) ( HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maui%27s_dolphin" \o "Maui's dolphin" Maui's dolphin), up to 9.5 m (30 ft) and 10 tonnes (9.8 long tons; 11 short tons) (the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Killer_whale" \o "Killer whale" orca or killer whale). They are found worldwide, mostly in the shallower seas of the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_shelves" \o "Continental shelves" continental shelves, and are carnivores, eating mostly  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish" \o "Fish" fish and  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Squid" \o "Squid" squid. The  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_(biology)" \o "Family (biology)" familie  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oceanic_dolphin" \o "Oceanic dolphin" Delphinidae is the largest in the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cetacea" \o "Cetacea" Cetacean order, and evolved relatively recently, about ten million years ago, during the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miocene" \o "Miocene" Miocene. Dolphins are among the most intelligent animals, and their often friendly appearance, an artifact of the "smile" of their mouth line, and seemingly playful attitude have made them very popular in  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_culture" \o "Human culture" human culture.AnatomyDolphins have a streamlined  HYPERLINK "http://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/fusiform" \o "wikt:fusiform" fusiform body, adapted for fast swimming. The tail fin, called the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/fluke" \o "wiktionary:fluke" fluke, is used for  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_locomotion" \o "Animal locomotion" propulsion, while the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pectoral_fin" \o "Pectoral fin" pectoral fins together with the entire tail section provide directional control. The  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dorsal_fin" \o "Dorsal fin" dorsal fin, in those species that have one, provides stability while swimming.The head contains the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Melon_(whale)" \o "Melon (whale)" melon, a round organ used for  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_echolocation" \o "Animal echolocation" echolocation. In many species, elongated jaws form a distinct beak; species such as the bottlenose have a curved mouth which looks like a fixed smile. Some specy have up to 250 teeth. Dolphins breathe through a  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blowhole_(biology)" \o "Blowhole (biology)" blowhole on top of their head. The  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vertebrate_trachea" \o "Vertebrate trachea" trachea is  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anterior" \o "Anterior" anterior to the brain. The  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolphin_brain" \o "Dolphin brain" dolphin brain is large and highly complex, and is different in structure from that of most land mammals.Unlike most mammals, dolphins do not have hair, except for a few hairs around the tip of their  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rostrum_(anatomy)" \o "Rostrum (anatomy)" rostrum (beak) which they lose shortly before or after birth. The only exception to this is the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boto" \o "Boto" Boto river dolphin, which has persistent small hairs on the rostrum Dolphins' reproductive organs are located on the underside of the body. Males have two slits, one concealing the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Penis" \o "Penis" penis and one further behind for the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anus" \o "Anus" anus. The female has one genital slit, housing the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vagina" \o "Vagina" vagina and the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anus" \o "Anus" anus. Though the exact methods used to achieve this are not known, dolphins can tolerate and recover from extreme injuries, such as  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shark" \o "Shark" shark bites. The  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healing" \o "Healing" healing process is rapid and even very deep wounds do not cause dolphins to  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemorrhage" \o "Hemorrhage" hemorrhage to death. Furthermore, even gaping wounds restore in such a way that the animal's body shape is restored, and  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infection" \o "Infection" infection of such large wounds seems rare. SensesMost dolphins have acute  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyesight" \o "Eyesight" eyesight, both in and out of the water, and they can hear frequencies ten times or more above the upper limit of adult human hearing. Though they have a small ear opening on each side of their head, it is believed hearing underwater is also, if not exclusively, done with the lower jaw, which conducts sound to the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_ear" \o "Middle ear" middle ear via a fat-filled cavity in the lower jaw bone. Hearing is also used for  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animal_echolocation" \o "Animal echolocation" echolocation, which all dolphins have. Dolphin teath are believed to function as antennae to receive incoming sound and to pinpoint the exact location of an object Beyond locating an object, echolocation also provides the animal with an idea on the object's shape and size, though how exactly this works is not yet understood. The  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_Dolphin" \o "Indus Dolphin" Indus Dolphin is effectively blind. This may be because not much light penetrates the waters of the  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indus_river" \o "Indus river" Indus river (due to suspended sediments), making eyes futile. The dolphin's sense of touch is also well-developed, with free nerve endings densely packed in the skin, especially around the snout, pectoral fins and genital area. However, dolphins lack an  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olfactory_nerve" \o "Olfactory nerve" olfactory nerve and lobes, and thus are believed to have no  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sense_of_smell" \o "Sense of smell" sense of smell. They do have a sense of  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taste" \o "Taste" taste and show preferences for certain kinds of fish. Since dolphins spend most of their time below the surface, tasting the water could function like smelling, in that substances in the water can signal the presence of objects that are not in the dolphin’s mouth.Though most dolphins do not have hair, they do have  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hair_follicle" \o "Hair follicle" hair follicles that may perform some sensory function The small hairs on the rostrum of the Boto river dolphin are believed to function as a  HYPERLINK "http://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somatosensory_system" \o "Somatosensory system" tactile sense possibly to compensate for the Boto's poor eyesight. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Dolphins_and_Humans.html" Dolphins and Humans Humans have been interacting with dolphins for as long as we have known of their existence. In the beginning, human interaction was mainly limited to hunting dolphins. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Dolphins_in_Captivity.html" Dolphins in Captivity Learn how dolphins in captivity behave compared with wild dolphins. Stories of boys riding dolphins date all the way back to Roman times, and even today scientific researchers sometimes ride wild dolphins they’re studying. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Learning_from_Dolphins.html" Learning from Dolphins While dolphin research is still in its infancy, the wealth of information that we may one day glean from these magnificent creatures is unimaginable. One lesson that we can learn from them today, however, could be a key to the survival of our civilization. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Dolphins_in_the_Military.html" Dolphins in the Military Dolphins in the military. We've heard for years that Dolphins are incredibly intelligent creatures. It turns out that they are even intelligent enough to serve in the United States Navy, and have been doing so since 1960. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Dolphins_in_Mythology.html" Dolphins in Mythology Dolphins in Mythology. Dolphins have been viewed as somehow magical for millennia by humans. They’re one of the only animals that appear to play, leaping out of the water and doing tricks, and the bottlenose dolphin even seems to grin widely at everything. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Dolphins_Rescuing_Humans.html" Dolphins Rescuing Humans Dolphins rescuing and helping humans. Dolphins are social animals, living in groups called pods and taking loving care of their babies. They’re also known to be very friendly to humans. HYPERLINK "http://www.dolphins-world.com/Dolphin_Assisted_Therapy.html" Dolphin Assisted Therapy Dolphin Assisted Therapy. Originating in 1978 by Dr. David Nathanson, Dolphin Assisted Therapy (DAT) has been used as a therapeutic approach to increase speech and motor skills in patients who have been diagnosed with developmental, physical, and/or emotional disabilities, such as mental retardation, Down syndrome, and autism.Dolphin Facts -animalstats- |  |  |  |  | MALE | FEMALE | YOUNG | SOCIAL UIT |  | bull | cow | calf | group |  | GROUP | HOME | HABITAT | FAVOURITE FOOD |  | pod | temperate and tropical oceans | ocean | fish, squid |  | TOP SPEED | LIFESPAN | AVG. LENGTH | AVG. WEIGHT |  | 35 mph | 30 - 40 years | male 8 feet female 7.5 feet | 500 - 650 pounds |  | ESTRUS | GESTATION | BIRTHWEIGHT | BIRTH LENGTH |  | unknown | 12 months | 40 - 60 pounds | 3 - 3.5 feet |  | BORN | # OF YOUNG | AT BIRTH | BABY COLOUR |  | tail first | 1 | immediately active | pailer than adult |  | WEANED | INDEPENDENT | MATURITY | ENDANGERED? |  | 12 - 16 months | 5 years | 6 - 10 years | no |  | Animal Facts Encyclopedia.com |  |  |  |  |
